## LLM (llm.md)

All deferred. Lands with the v3.5 ACP-client + bundled-agent milestone in `design.md` build order. Architectural decisions (ACP-only, three feature types, ToS posture, etc.) live in the spec; the slugs below are concrete implementable features.

| Slug | Status | Notes |
| ---- | ------ | ----- |
| `llm-core-module` | done | `core/src/llm.rs` — `LlmClient` trait (`chat` / `chat_stream`) + `GraniteLlmClient` wrapping graniet/`llm`; `ProviderConfig` carries backend/model/api-key-env/base-url/limits; module discipline holds (only place the `llm` crate is imported). `MockLlmClient` for downstream tests. TOML loader for `llm-providers-config` is the next slug |
| `llm-providers-config` | done | `core/src/config.rs` — `[llm]` section in the standard hiker config (per-vault `vault/.hiker/config.toml` with user-scope fallback, deep-merged like every other section): top-level `enabled` (reserved for `llm-disable-mode`), `[llm.provider]` (backend / model / api_key_env / api_key / base_url), `[llm.limits]` (max_tokens / timeout_secs), `[llm.agent]` (iteration_cap / tool_timeout_secs), `[llm.audit] log_full_prompt`. Strict-load validates the section. `core::llm::provider_config_from` + `GraniteLlmClient::from_config` build a usable client from the loaded section. API keys come from one of two sources, in precedence order: literal `[llm.provider].api_key` (user-scope TOML only — eligibility list refuses vault-scope writes so a synced vault can't carry the secret) takes precedence over `api_key_env` when set. The settings pane hides the `api_key` row whenever the section's scope toggle is at `[Vault]`. Empty + empty = no key on the builder (correct for local Ollama). |
| `llm-basic-agent-loop` | done | `core/src/agent.rs` — `run_turn(input, client, dispatcher, cfg, events_tx, cancel)` runs one user-message → terminal-response cycle. `LlmClient` extended with `chat_with_tools` (returns `ToolChatResponse` carrying text + `Vec<ToolCall>`); message history carries `tool_calls` / `tool_results` so multi-turn tool-use is coherent. Tool dispatch goes through the injected `ToolDispatcher` trait — the MCP wiring still lives in `agent-tool-routing-via-mcp` (separate slug). 6 unit tests cover terminal turn, tool-use turn, cap-hit, tool timeout, tool failure, and cancel. |
| `agent-tool-routing-via-mcp` | done | `mcp-server/src/agent_bridge.rs::McpAgentDispatcher` implements `core::agent::ToolDispatcher` against an `Arc<HikerHandler>` exposed via `McpServerHandle::agent_handler()`. `HikerHandler::dispatch_tool(name, args_json)` is the new in-process entry — same `_inner()` helpers the rmcp tools call, same audit log, same error model. `agent_tool_defs()` advertises the seven tools with `schemars`-derived JSON Schemas; the host's chat module injects this dispatcher into `run_turn`. |
| `agent-event-stream-shape` | done | `core/src/agent.rs::AgentEvent` covers the 11 spec variants (`ToolCallArgsDelta` added with the streaming variant). Emitted on `tokio::mpsc::Sender<AgentEvent>` from `core::agent`; the host re-emits each on the chat events channel; `app/src/chat/render.rs` dispatches by `kind` to render text deltas, tool-call cards, the Continue/Stop row, errors, and finish-reason rows. |
| `agent-chat-command-surface` | done | Backend: the host's chat module — `chat_send` / `chat_continue` / `chat_stop` / `chat_cancel` commands; `VaultSession::chat: Arc<ChatRegistry>` holds the spec's `Arc<Mutex<HashMap<TurnId, TurnState>>>` shape (history, tools, system prompt, `StopSignal`, `in_flight` flag, finish flag). Each `chat_send` / `chat_continue` spawns a per-turn tokio task (refused under the registry lock if `in_flight`) that drives `run_turn`, forwards each event through a tiny event-pump task, and writes the new history back so a follow-up resume sees the latest state; an RAII guard wrapping the spawned task clears `in_flight` (and GCs the registry on terminal finish) regardless of how the task exits, so a panic mid-loop can't leak the entry. `chat_continue` re-invokes `run_turn` directly with `user_message: None` per the spec — the cap reset falls out because `run_turn`'s cap is per-call, no empty user message is appended. `chat_stop` fires `StopSignal::user_halt` (→ `FinishReason::UserHalted`); `chat_cancel` fires `StopSignal::cancel` (→ `FinishReason::Cancelled`). Frontend wired up in `app/src/chat/render.rs` (+ `send.rs`) — see `chat-panel-*` rows. |
| `agent-iteration-cap-prompt` | done | core-side: `run_turn` returns `FinishReason::CapHit`, emits `AgentEvent::IterationCapHit`. UI-side: `app/src/chat/render.rs` renders the spec's `"Agent has made N tool calls — [Continue] [Stop]"` row; Continue invokes `chat_continue(turn_id)` (which re-enters `run_turn` with the same accumulated history and a fresh cap budget — exactly "resets to 10 more"); Stop invokes `chat_stop(turn_id)`. |
| `agent-tool-call-timeout` | done | `core/src/agent.rs::dispatch_with_timeout` wraps each `ToolDispatcher::dispatch` call in `tokio::time::timeout` using `cfg.tool_timeout_secs` (default 30 from `[llm.agent] tool_timeout_secs`). On timeout: emits a `ToolResult { ok: false, summary: "tool timed out" }` event and feeds the same shape into history so the model can recover. Never bubbles as a turn-killing error. Cancellation is interleaved via `tokio::select!` so a `chat_cancel` mid-dispatch unblocks the wait without leaking the dispatcher task to its natural completion. |
| `llm-acp-client-optional` | done | `core/src/acp.rs:1`, `core/src/config.rs:137`, the host chat module, `app/src/panels/settings/mod.rs` | `core::acp` module spawns external ACP agent process, creates session with hiker MCP server auto-attached via `McpServer::Http`, translates ACP events to `AgentEvent`. `[acp].agent` config key ("none" / "claude-code" / "goose"), settings pane dropdown. `chat_send`/`chat_continue` branch to `spawn_acp_turn()` when agent != "none". Session resume via ACP's `session/load` not yet supported — agent starts fresh each turn |

MCP server stays available; deterministic pipelines (indexing/embedding/search/clustering build/wikilinks/op-log/sync) keep running. User-scope default; per-vault override via section scope toggle. Live-applied |

Unknown placeholders pass through verbatim rather than blanking, so a partially-customized prompt doesn't silently lose data. Each shipped default starts with an HTML-comment header naming its placeholders. |

Bumping the bundled default upstream surfaces as a stale-feature name from `staleness()`; the user file is never clobbered. the host (`open_vault_at_inner`) calls `staleness()` once per session and emits both a `tracing::warn!` and an `AgentLog` row (`surface = "core::agent"`, `status = "stale_prompt"`) per stale feature, so the future Prompts-tab can read either surface. |

The shared writer means `[mcp.audit] log_full_input` and `[llm.audit] log_full_prompt` land their rows in the same daily file. Background / fan-out / ACP surfaces will plug in by passing `Some(AgentAudit { ... })` (or constructing their own `AuditEntry`) when those slugs land. |

Resolves the rel-path from the tool result (or args fallback); for multi-note touches, header-click opens a picker. Honors `file-switch-guard-dirty`. Pairs with the trail-from-llm-trace future feature for query auditing |
| `chat-retry-from-user-message` | planned | — | circular-arrow icon-button on each user message; click rolls session back to immediately before that message and starts a fresh turn with the same text. Disabled while a turn is in flight. Available on both `core::agent` and `core::acp` paths |
| `chat-retry-truncates-session-history` | planned | — | retry trims every turn at-or-after the retried user message from `SessionState`; the session markdown file (under `chats/` per `chat-session-markdown-store`) is rewritten to match (markdown is truth); one history frame appended for the truncation. No hidden-branch archive in v1 |
| `chat-retry-resubmits-verbatim` | planned | — | retried turn carries the exact original user message text including `@`-mentions; context blocks (active note, `@<note-path>`, `@selection`) re-resolve at submit time. Message is not prefilled into the input — edit-and-resend is a separate future affordance |
| `chat-retry-command` | planned | — | new `chat_retry(session_id, user_message_id) -> Result<TurnId>` on the agent command surface. Implementation: truncate state + markdown → emit `TurnStarted` for the new id → invoke the same turn-driver as `chat_send`. No confirmation modal — the UI rollback animation conveys destructiveness |
| `chat-retry-acp` | planned | — | ACP path maps retry onto ending the current `session/prompt` line and starting a new one with the truncated history; falls back to spawning a fresh ACP session seeded with the pre-retry turns when the configured agent doesn't model history truncation natively |
| `chat-edit-and-resend` | planned | — | deferred — pencil-icon affordance on a user message drops its text back into the input for editing; same destructive trim as retry, but the resend is manual |
| `chat-retry-branches-session` | planned | — | deferred — keep the discarded tail as a named branch of the session so the user can compare outcomes |
| `chat-regenerate-assistant-message` | planned | — | deferred — regenerate-this-response button on an assistant bubble; same `chat_retry` plumbing anchored on the prior user turn |
| `chat-panel-default-height` | done | `core::config::VaultConfig::chat_height` (default `0.30`); `core::config::ELIGIBLE_VAULT` accepts the new `vault.chat_height` key with `ValueType::UnitFraction` validation (rejects values outside `[0.0, 1.0]`); `set_setting` persists via `toml_edit` so user comments survive. `app/src/chat/render.rs` reads `vault.chat_height` after settings load and applies the persisted fraction on vault open. |
| `chat-panel-expand-to-editor` | partial | `app/src/chat/render.rs` (expand button → open agent tab), `app/src/panels/agent.rs` (`agent`-kind tab body), `app/src/workbench_host.rs` (agent branch + agent-key autosave restore) | basic wiring landed: expand button → opens an `agent`-kind tab; the bottom chat region + resize handle hide while it's open. **Partial**: full expand-to-editor experience (session body rendered in tab, chat-region collapse/restore on agent-tab open/close) is still planned; current wiring is the foundations. |
| `chat-session-import-storage-path` | planned | imports land at `vault/<chats_dir>/imported/<source>-<YYYY-MM-DD>-<id>.md` — the visible `imported/` subfolder of `chats/`; same on-disk format as native sessions per `chat-session-markdown-store`; ordinary indexed notes, no `.hiker/` carve-out. Storage layer ready: `core::sessions::list` / `app::chat::session::discover` recurse into `chats/imported/`, and the sessions migration relocates legacy `.hiker/sessions/imported/` |
| `chat-session-import-converters` | planned | `core::sessions::import::<source>` per format; v1 candidates: Claude Code transcript JSON, Codex export, ChatGPT data-export JSON, Goose session log; preserves tool-call structure into hiker's `hiker-tool-call` block when the source carries it, otherwise lossy-text |
| `chat-session-import-sidecar` | planned | entry points: `hiker session import <path>` CLI + settings-UI affordance; sniff → convert → write → index; idempotent on `(source, source_id)` so re-imports update in place |
| `chat-session-imported-visibility` | planned | imported sessions are ordinary visible notes at `chats/imported/`, indexed + search-reachable; Vault mode groups them as a distinct "Imported sessions" provenance bucket (`vault-view-source-groups`) — no hide toggle |
| `chat-session-respawn-from-import` | planned | "Continue as chat" affordance on imported sessions creates a new native session at `vault/<chats_dir>/<date>-<new-id>.md` seeded with the imported turns (text-only by the lossy resume convention); basic chat agent picks up using current `[llm]` provider; new session's frontmatter records `respawned_from: imported/<source>-<date>-<id>.md`; ACP path seeds via Embedded Resource ContentBlock |

Today's sharp top border looks messy where it meets the sections region. Pairs with `chat-panel-pinned-bottom` (the chat region the shadow applies to) and `chat-panel-expand-to-editor` (agent-tab body needs the same treatment). CSS-only; no behavior change |
| `chat-new-session-reveals-panel` | done | `app/src/actions.rs::new_chat_session` calls `crate::chat::session::create_new`, then `ensure_panel_visible(PANEL_CHAT)` to insert the chat panel into the dock if it was removed, and sets `state.workbench.secondary_side_bar.visible = true` because the chat surface lives in the workbench's secondary side bar in the egui layout — re-inserting alone doesn't un-collapse a hidden side bar |


## Task queue (task-queue.md)

Unified work queue for non-interactive LLM work — note mutations, RAPTOR / fan-out, background single-shots. Producers submit; consumers are the in-process direct-LLM worker and any rmcp client (external HTTP, or the basic chat agent in-process when `expose_to_chat_agent` is on). Lands as a sibling to `core::llm` and `core::agent`. Spec is `task-queue.md`.

| Slug | Status | Evidence | Notes |
| ---- | ------ | -------- | ----- |
| `task-queue-core-module` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs` (`Queue`, `Slot`, broadcast event channel, lease table) | new `core::tasks` module (queue + lease table + event emitter) wired into `VaultSession` at the host |
| `task-queue-scope-non-chat` | partial | `core/src/tasks.rs` (`TaskKind` enum); `submit_note_mutation` producer in the host | note-mutation menu (the first non-chat producer) now flows through the queue; RAPTOR / auto-tag / summary-on-save producers are still planned, so the slug stays partial until they land |
| `task-queue-task-shape` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs` (`Task`, `TaskKind`, `TaskShape`, `Priority`, `TaskPayload`) | exhaustive `TaskKind` enum + `TaskShape::{Direct, Agent}` + `Priority::{Low, Normal, High}` |
| `task-queue-shape-routing` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs::pick_next` (skips `Agent` shape for direct lane), `Queue::checkout_mcp` (no shape filter beyond opt-in) | direct worker only takes `Direct`; MCP clients can take either shape |
| `task-queue-io-worker-lane` | planned | a dedicated in-process worker for long-running non-LLM I/O (web crawl, `crawl-task-queue-lane`); distinct from the direct-LLM drain and the synchronous `NonLlmHandlers` path; never an MCP client; no `output_schema`; on the queue for lease/progress/cancel/visibility; per-page tasks roll up via `task-queue-task-grouping` |
| `task-queue-priority-tiers` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs::Priority::rank`, `pick_next` ordering | strict High/Normal/Low ordering with FIFO inside via `submitted_at` |
| `task-queue-raptor-summarize` | partial | Enum variant + payload (Sprint A) plus a producer side (Sprint D): `cluster_regenerate_names` submits one task per non-user-edited cluster node at `Priority::Normal`. **Gap:** no concrete LLM-backed worker that consumes the task yet — the direct-LLM drain receives it but the worker that resolves members + writes the summary back through `Trees::set_summary` + `reset_churn` is the next chunk. Retry rides producer-side error handling |
| `task-queue-raptor-triage-match` | done | Enum variant + payload (Sprint A), producer side (Sprint D Phase 1) — `cluster_triage_enqueue` submits one `RaptorTriageMatch` task per saved-as-triage tree at `Priority::Normal` — and consumer side (Sprint D fix-up): the direct worker checks a `NonLlmHandlers` side-channel before falling back to the LLM (`core/src/tasks.rs::NonLlmHandlers::try_handle` + `drive_one_task`). `the host's `DirectWorkerHandlers`` implements the trait and dispatches `RaptorTriageMatch` to `core::suggest::triage_match` with the session-scoped trees / vault / staging / store handles and the live `[suggestions.triage]` config — same classifier the synchronous on-save fast path uses. Payload schema carries `tree_id` + `source_path` on the variant itself (no prompt body required). Author class is hardcoded `User` for now — see `triage-author-class` (partial) for the agent-author lift. Cancel-by-source-path remains a producer-side concern |
| `raptor-summarize-sample-merge` | partial | `core/src/cluster.rs::plan_sample_merge` + `SampleMergePlan { Single, SampleAndMerge { batches }, TooLarge { member_count } }` | pure planner: members partitioned into 30-per-batch siblings above the threshold, with a 300-member cap that routes to `TooLarge` (producer skips summarization for that cluster — no fallback path). Constants `SAMPLE_MERGE_BATCH_THRESHOLD` / `BATCH_SIZE` / `MEMBER_CAP` exported for producer reuse. **Gap:** no fan-in machinery in `core::tasks` yet — sibling batches submit independently; the merge step is the producer's responsibility (matches `task-queue-task-grouping` being deferred). When the producer lands, each batch is a `RaptorSummarize` task; the merge is a separate `RaptorSummarize` whose payload aggregates the partial summaries |
| `task-queue-lifecycle` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs::TaskState`, `tick_maintenance` (lease-expiry requeue) | `Queued → Leased → Completed/Failed/Cancelled` with expiry-requeue path |
| `task-queue-terminal-retention` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs::tick_maintenance` (GC past `terminal_retention_secs`); ticked every 2s from the host | terminal rows kept for `terminal_retention_secs` (default 60) then GC'd |
| `task-queue-lease-timeout` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs::checkout_mcp` (clamps to `[1, max_secs]`), `heartbeat` extends, `tick_maintenance` requeues | external checkout stamps a lease + heartbeat extends; expiry requeues |
| `task-queue-submit-handle` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs::TaskHandle::await_outcome` | producer gets a handle awaitable for `TaskOutcome`; drop is *not* implicit cancel (deviates from spec — see notes) |

| `task-queue-stale-lease-rejection` | done | `core/src/tasks.rs::Queue::cancel` flips state to `Cancelled` while resolving the producer handle; subsequent `submit_result`/`fail` return `QueueError::StaleLease`; MCP layer maps that to error code `1006` | producer handle resolves immediately; client learns at submit time |

| `task-queue-acp-via-mcp-only` | done | no ACP→queue wiring in `core::acp` (which doesn't exist yet); the queue only sees rmcp clients | enforced by absence — the queue is reachable only via `Queue::*` (in-process) and `task_*` MCP tools |

## Diff (diff.md)

Unified diff primitive `DiffLayer` produces hunks + decorations from two ropes; consumed by every diff surface (dirty-buffer toggle, `.ops` history browser, history-version review, inline agent patch-review, whole-file proposal review). Diff is a mode of the unified `buffer` tab kind, not a separate tab kind; `DiffSource` picks the comparison target.

| Slug | Status | Notes |
| ---- | ------ | ----- |
| `diff-core-module` | done | `core/src/diff.rs` (`compute`, `DiffResult`, `DiffHunk`, `DiffLine`, `DiffOp`). Pure text → diff using `similar`'s `TextDiff::from_lines` + `grouped_ops(3)`; `similar` confined to the module |
| `diff-layer` | done | `editor/editor-diff/src/lib.rs` (`DiffLayer`, `DiffOwner`). Constructor takes base + current + owner; `decorations(line_height, theme, intraline)` runs the existing `unified_decorations_opts` and returns a `DecorationSet`. Every diff path in the app routes through this — `panels/buffer/diff_overlay.rs` (diff is a mode of the editor tab via `DiffSource` in `app/src/tab.rs`) |
| `diff-as-mode` | done | the editor tab carries `Option<DiffSource>`; `diff_overlay::compute` resolves the source and pushes a `DiffLayer`-derived layer onto the same editor widget. Diff toggle in the toolbar flips the active tab's `diff` field; no separate diff-tab kind |
| `diff-source-enum` | done | `app/src/tab.rs` (`DiffSource::{ Disk { path }, Empty, HistoryVersion { op_id, path }, PendingProposal { proposal_id } }`). Each variant resolves to a base rope in `diff_overlay::resolve_source_text` (on-disk read, op-log version via `op_writes`, or empty) |
| `diff-layer-owner` | done | `DiffOwner::{ Index, Pending, Agent, HistoryVersion, Manual }` (`editor/editor-diff/src/lib.rs`). `diff_overlay::compute` picks the owner from buffer state (Agent for hydrated proposals) or the DiffSource shape. Agent owner additionally emits per-hunk Accept/Reject ActionRow widgets via `attach_agent_hunk_widgets` |
| `diff-layer-hunk-widgets` | done | `app/src/panels/buffer/diff_overlay.rs` — `attach_agent_hunk_widgets` (Accept/Reject per agent hunk) + `attach_history_version_hunk_widgets` (Restore per history-version hunk). Click ids map through `DiffOverlay.click_map` to `HunkAction::{Accept, Reject, Restore}`. Buffer panel dispatches: `handle_hunk_accept` / `handle_hunk_reject` walk the contributing proposals; `handle_hunk_restore` splices the history version's byte-range text into the on-disk content via `vault.write_file_checked` and appends a `Modified` audit row |
| `diff-viewer-input-shape` | done | `app/src/tab.rs` (`DiffSource` — the diff input). `{ before, after }` — actions + close move out of the renderer's contract since each consumer's preview pane owns its own banner + lifecycle |
| `diff-viewer-line-unified` | done | whole-file paint with red/green per-line decorations over `editor_core::diff::lines`; the renderer paints full file context with changes inline. The between-hunks `⋯` code path is in place but unreachable until `diff-viewer-grouped-hunks` lands |
| `diff-viewer-grouped-hunks` | planned | future `core::diff` entry point emits multiple hunks with bounded context (~3 lines per side); `⋯` separators + click-to-expand land then. Triggered by large-file ergonomics or `mcp-tool-diff`. Wire format already accommodates a list of hunks |
| `editor-toolbar-mode-controls` | done | centered slot between flex spacers; populated with mode-specific label + icon-only buttons whenever the active buffer's tab carries a `DiffSource` that owns verbs (snapshot restore, proposal accept/reject). Idempotent rebuild via `replaceChildren()` on every transition |
| `diff-viewer-respects-dirty-source` | done | entering diff mode is not a buffer swap — the tab's `current` is unchanged, decorations layer on top — so `file-switch-guard-dirty` doesn't fire and dirty-buffer state is preserved across mode toggles |
| `editor-diff-vs-disk-toggle` | done | toolbar diff toggle. Greyed when buffer is clean *and* `DiffSource` is `Disk(path)` *and* the path exists. Click toggles the tab's diff mode against the current `DiffSource` (default `Disk(path)`). Right-click opens a source picker: `Diff against on-disk`, `Show changes…` (submenu), future sources |
| `editor-show-changes-menu` | done | `app/src/panels/buffer/show_changes.rs` (`show_diff_source_menu`). Right-click on the diff toolbar button opens "Diff against on-disk" + "Show changes…" submenu of up to 20 recent accepted ops for the path (timestamp / op-kind / author, via `op_writes::path_history`). Selecting opens (or focuses) a history-version tab keyed on the op's `op_id`; "Browse all…" routes to the activity-row detail page. Restore-this-hunk verb on HistoryVersion hunks pending (`diff-layer-hunk-widgets`) |
| `cli-diff` | planned | `hiker diff <path> [<change-id>]` over `editor_core::diff::lines`; lands when the CLI is fleshed out |
| `mcp-tool-diff` | planned | reserved as deferred — agents can already retrieve two blobs (`get_note` + `change_content`) and reason over them |
| `activity-detail-diff-between-versions` | planned | multi-select two activity rows + "Diff selected"; opens a `buffer` tab with `current = content_at(id_a)`, `diff = Some(HistoryVersion { op_id: id_b })`; depends on activity-detail multi-select |
| `diff-viewer-split-view` | planned | side-by-side layout option on `DiffLayer` (two editor widgets sharing the same hunks). Same primitive; different layout |
| `diff-viewer-intraline` | done | character-level highlights inside paired delete/insert lines. Renderer adds intra mark decorations on top of the line backgrounds |
| `diff-intraline-core-pair` | removed | dead intraline-IPC scaffolding in `hiker_core::diff` (zero callers) deleted per `scratch/substrate_decision.md` safe-now cleanup. Intraline highlighting lives in the editor renderer (`diff-intraline-render-marks`) over `editor_core::diff`, which is unaffected |

| `view-intraline-diff-toggle` | done | "Intraline diff highlights" View-menu entry flips `editor.intraline_diff`; affects every `DiffLayer` consumer. Toggling while a diff is rendered re-runs the compute |
| `diff-viewer-three-way` | planned | third rope slot on `DiffLayer` for merge / drift-conflict resolution; anchors `drift-conflict-modal`'s deferred "open diff" option |
| `diff-viewer-ignore-whitespace` | planned | toggle on the `compute` call to normalize whitespace before diffing |
| `diff-viewer-export-patch` | planned | "copy as unified-diff patch" affordance |


## Op log (op-log.md)

The local editing & history substrate: a plain `.md` file per document (path is identity), edited as plain text with an editor-native anchored overlay for agent review (`ChangeSet`/`map_pos`). Three composable text views — `accepted` (committed `.md`), `working` (editor buffer), `pending(session)` (an agent session's anchored edits). History is per-save text frames in a per-document `.ops` file (`materialize_at`); the only database is the regenerable `index.db`. Sync is a separate pluggable transport (`sync.md`/`git.md`). `core::oplog` owns the substrate; producers wire through `core::ops::op_writes`.

| Slug | Status | Notes |
| ---- | ------ | ----- |
| `op-log-path-identity` | partial | identity is the vault path; the per-doc `.ops`/`.pending` and history follow the path, renames move them. Removing the residual ULID `doc_id` + `doc-index.db` path map is tracked in `bug-remove-yrs-substrate` |
| `op-log-sidecar-document` | done | non-md sources are edited via their sidecar `<src>.md`; the source is read-only to the log. `core/src/ops/op_writes.rs` create/bootstrap treats the sidecar as the document |
| `op-log-external-handle-deferred` | planned | external-source pointer documents (a watched file outside the vault) under path-as-identity; deferred |
| `op-log-layered-model` | partial | the editable buffer is `accepted + working`; the agent's `pending` renders on top as the review overlay. `materialize_working`/`materialize_review` seams + `app/src/panels/buffer/editor_binding.rs`. Making the overlay editor-native (anchored ranges via `map_pos`) is `op-log-three-way-overlay` |
| `op-log-working-layer` | done | the user's uncommitted edits over `accepted`; `core/src/oplog/working.rs` (`apply_working_edit`/`materialize_working`/`discard_working`/`has_working_edits`) + `commit_working` (Save folds into `accepted`). Tested `working_edit_shows_in_buffer_not_on_disk`, `commit_working_folds_into_accepted` |
| `op-log-three-way-overlay` | planned | the agent overlay anchors via the editor's own `ChangeSet`/`map_pos` (exact remap). Ride the existing diff-based overlay (`app/src/panels/buffer/diff_overlay.rs` re-diffs each frame); accept builds a `Set` applied as a `Transaction`; drift is a base-context re-diff check |
| `op-log-agent-session-view` | planned | the agent reads/writes a session text (`accepted` + its own edits), materialized in memory; pending hunks derive on demand by diffing `accepted` vs the session text. Reads-after-writes consistent, dependent edits compose, never blocks on acceptance |
| `op-log-pending-patch` | partial | agent edits staged as a per-session set anchored against `accepted`; accept folds a hunk into `working`, reject drops it. `stage_pending`/`accept_pending`/`reject_pending`. The anchoring becomes editor-native under `op-log-three-way-overlay` |
| `op-log-pending-survives-restart` | done | `core/src/oplog/store.rs::{save_pending, load_pending}` persist the pending set to `<path>.pending`; `ensure_loaded` reconstitutes on reopen. Tested `pending_survives_restart` |

Pure detection + revert args in `app/src/panels/buffer/conflict.rs` (tested); verbs wire through `diff_overlay.rs` (`conflict_row`) → `patch_review.rs`. Feeds the unified conflict surface (`sync-unified-conflict-surface`) |

Tested `ops/tests.rs::auto_reject_on_drift_flips_a_drifted_op` |

Tested `ops/tests.rs::hunk_accept_applies_only_overlapping_ops` |

Collapsing to this layout (dropping `.yrs`/`.yrslog`, `doc-index.db`, `oplog_meta.db`) is `bug-remove-yrs-substrate` |

Tested `materialize_at_reconstructs_each_accepted_version`, `materialize_at_records_tombstone_transition`, `materialize_at_survives_restart` |

## Patch review (patch-review.md)

Inline review of pending agent edits on the active buffer. The buffer renders `materialize(accepted + working)`; the agent's `pending` edits render on top as a read-only overlay — the diff against `materialize(accepted + working + pending(session))` rendered as hunks via `DiffLayer`, recomputed from the two ropes each frame (no anchor tracker). Per-hunk accept folds the contributing edits into `working`; reject drops them. Substrate in `op-log.md`.

| Slug | Status | Notes |
| ---- | ------ | ----- |
| `patch-review-buffer-state` | done | `app/src/panels/buffer/editor_binding.rs` runs `log.materialize_review(rel)` off `op_writes::review_materializations` → sets `buffer.agent_proposal = review` and `agent_base = accepted`; clears `agent_base` to `None` when the two are equal (no pending ops in scope). Re-runs each frame the buffer is shown and after every flip. The `Buffer` carries `active_session` to scope the overlay |
| `patch-review-buffer-is-view` | done | the buffer rope is a pure re-materialization off the substrate: open re-materializes via `editor_binding`'s `materialize_review`, close discards the rope (`close_tab` buffer drop), reopen re-materializes fresh |
| `patch-review-diff-layer` | done | `app/src/panels/buffer/diff_overlay.rs::compute` builds `DiffLayer { base: agent_base, current, owner: Agent }` and pushes decorations onto the editor's decoration stack. Same primitive powers diff toggle and snapshot/history viewer |
| `patch-review-per-hunk-accept` | done | `diff_overlay.rs::attach_agent_hunk_widgets` resolves each hunk's `current_range` → pending op ids via `op_writes::ops_in_hunk(rel, session, start, end)`; the Accept/Reject click routes through `app/src/panels/buffer/patch_review.rs::{apply_hunk_accept, apply_hunk_reject}` → `op_writes::flip_op_status`. After the flip the next-frame editor binding re-materializes the pending-view (`editor_binding::materialize_review`) so resolved hunks disappear |
| `patch-review-coexisting-edits` | done | user typing produces accepted ops; the diff `base` vs `current` excludes/includes them symmetrically so user typing produces no self-diff hunks — it sits on both sides of the diff base |
| `patch-review-batch-grouping` | planned | ops sharing `metadata.batch_id` (e.g. multiple `Replace` ops from one `edit_note` call) get a thin gutter connector between sibling hunks. No coupled behavior; per-hunk accept/reject independent |
| `patch-review-multi-session` | done | `patch_review.rs::pill_meta` tallies pending ops per `session_id` off `OpLog::pending_ops`; when >1 session has pending ops the pill (`patch_review_pill.rs`) renders one selectable `Session <id>: N hunks` row each. Clicking sets `Buffer.active_session` and re-materializes the pending-view scoped to that session (`review_materializations` / `ops_in_hunk` take the session) |
| `patch-review-file-pill` | done | `app/src/panels/buffer/patch_review_pill.rs` consumes `Vec<HunkInfo>` from the diff overlay; renders `N agent hunks (M drifted)` + Accept-all / Reject-all / Next-hunk + per-session rows. Bulk verbs flip pending ops through `op_writes::flip_op_status` (Accept-all skips drifted, Reject-all covers them); the next-frame editor binding re-materializes via `editor_binding::materialize_review`. Drifted count from `is_pending_drifted` |
| `patch-review-conflicted-hunk-display` | planned | conflicted proposals (hydration-time apply failures, or disk-side state from `staging-drift-eager-recheck` before hydration) surface via the file-pill's `(M conflicted)` suffix. They don't render as hunks in the inline view |
| `patch-review-conflicted-accept-disabled` | partial | inline hunk overlay: `diff_overlay.rs::attach_agent_hunk_widgets` greys the per-hunk Accept button (`enabled: false`) and labels the row `Hunk N (drifted)` when any contributing op is drifted (`OpLog::is_pending_drifted`); Reject stays active. The file pill surfaces the `(M drifted)` count. Gap: the file-pill popover + `Changes`-tab drift-reason tooltip are still planned |
| `patch-review-conflicted-list-popover` | planned | clicking the `(M conflicted)` suffix on the file pill opens a popover listing each conflicted proposal with `[Reject]` and `View` (opens the proposal in diff mode against `Empty`) |
| `patch-review-decorations` | planned | umbrella slug for the inline rendering of pending edits. Implementation now rides `patch-review-diff-layer` rather than a custom decoration provider |
| `note-open-routes-to-pending-review` | planned | when `openFile` resolves a path with at least one pending whole-file proposal, the open lands in the whole-file review surface (per `write-note-review-surface`); pending `edit_note` proposals trigger hydration into plain editing |
| `write-note-review-surface` | done | whole-file proposals are pending whole-body `Replace{anchor:None}` / `Create` edits listed via `op_writes::list_whole_file_proposals` (per-frame cache `ui_cache.whole_file_proposals`). Open as `TabKind::Editor { buffer: PendingProposal{ proposal_id, target_path }, diff: Some(Disk{target_path}) }`; `editor_pane::ensure_readonly_buffer_loaded` + `diff_overlay::resolve_source_text` load the proposed content via `op_writes::proposal_materializations`. Accept/reject route through `op_writes::flip_op_status` |
| `write-note-review-mode-label` | planned | label reads `Review rewrite` (target path exists on disk) vs. `Review new note` (target path doesn't), with muted origin suffix (`· chat` / `· batch` / `· trail`) |
| `write-note-review-blocks-on-dirty` | planned | Accept refuses with modal when the live buffer for the target path is dirty. Reject works regardless |
| `write-note-pending-banner` | planned | thin amber strip above the editor when a buffer tab is in plain editing and its path has a pending whole-file proposal. `Review` button switches the tab into the whole-file review surface |
| `write-note-review-conflicted-display` | done | drift is read off the op log (`OpLog::is_pending_drifted`, surfaced on `WholeFileProposal.drifted`). Accept is disabled with a drift reason in the tooltip on both the whole-file review toolbar (`render_readonly_source_toolbar`) and the pending-rewrite banner (`pending_rewrite_banner`); proposed content still renders; Reject + View stay active |
| `editor-action-row-primitive` | done | `BlockKind::ActionRow { label, glyph, tone, buttons }` in `editor-core::decoration` with `ActionButton`, `ActionButtonStyle` (Primary/Danger/Neutral), `ActionTone` (Normal/Warning/Conflicted). Rendered by `paint_action_row` in `editor-egui`; each enabled button registers a per-rect `ClickZone` with `ClickAction::WidgetClick(button.id)`. Pure-data primitive used by `diff-layer-hunk-widgets` |
| `editor-inline-widget-display` | done | `InlineWidget` trait carries `fn display() -> Option<InlineWidgetDisplay>`. When `Some`, the egui painter renders the supplied `{ text, bg, fg, strikethrough }` as the segment galley + bg fill instead of the bordered placeholder |
| `editor-end-of-line-widget` | done | `build_line_layout` collects `InlineWidget` decorations whose `range.start == line_byte_end` into a trailing-segment list rather than clipping them to zero width |
| `patch-review-cross-file-deferred` | planned | multi-file `edit_note` workflows; depends on a future `multi_edit_note` tool surface |
| `patch-review-three-way-deferred` | planned | true three-way merge for whole-file accept-while-dirty; rides `diff-viewer-three-way` |
| `patch-review-per-agent-attribution-deferred` | planned | per-edit badges showing which agent issued which hunk; useful only with concurrent multi-agent proposals |
| `patch-review-hunk-keybind-deferred` | planned | global keybind for next/previous hunk in the active buffer; the file pill's Next button covers the immediate need |


## Changes log (op-log.md)

User-facing surfaces over the op log: history queries, rollback verbs, the merged activity feed. Substrate + projection spec live in `op-log.md` ("History"). These surfaces are projections over the `.ops` history frames plus content materialization via `op_writes::content_at`.

| Slug | Status | Notes |
| ---- | ------ | ----- |
| `changes-query-api` | done | `core/src/activity.rs::AcceptedFeed` — thin projection over `OpLog::query_metadata` returning the `ChangeRow`/`ChangeOp` DTOs (defined in the same module): `recent`/`recent_by_author`/`history_for_path` over accepted ops, `op_kind_to_change_op` maps `create→Created`, `replace`/`set_frontmatter→Modified`, `tombstone→Deleted`, `rename→Renamed`, `rename_from` carried; the version id preserved in the row's `metadata` (the `id: i64` field holds `timestamp_ms`), `is_current` stamped over the window; rows keyed by path. Per-path version lists / content read through `core/src/ops/op_writes.rs::{path_history, content_at}`. Tested `core/src/activity.rs` |
| `changes-rollback-helper` | done | `app/src/panels/home.rs::rollback_change` pulls the prior content via `op_writes::previous_accepted_content` and writes it back through `op_writes::user_save` — a fresh `user` op that becomes the newest accepted version (the original op stays in the log). Version restore (`app/src/panels/buffer/mod.rs::restore_snapshot_to_disk` / `apply_hunk_restore`) reads `content_at_op` and re-saves through `user_save` the same way |
| `activity-feed-merged` | done | `core/src/activity.rs`, `core/src/lib.rs` (mod), the host (`VaultSession.activity` + `activity_list*` commands), `app/src/panels/changes.rs` (activity detail uses `activity_list`) | backend-merged feed projects over `core::oplog` — accepted ops → `Payload::Change(ChangeRow)`, pending ops → `Payload::Pending(PendingItem)`; one stream, no frontend reconciliation |
| `activity-feed-module` | done | `core/src/activity.rs` (`Activity::new(Arc<OpLog>)`), the host (`activity: Arc<Activity>` on `VaultSession`, constructed from `oplog.clone()` in `bootstrap.rs`) | pure projection — no fs/db writes; depends only on `Arc<OpLog>`. Accepted via `AcceptedFeed`, pending via `OpLog::all_pending_ops`. The `ChangeRow`/`ChangeOp`/`AcceptedFeed` DTOs live in this module. Tested `core/src/activity.rs::tests` |
| `activity-feed-unified-item` | done | `core/src/activity.rs` (`ActivityItem`, `ActivityPayload`, `ActivitySummary`) | tagged enum `kind: "change" \| "staging"` with newtype-variant flattening; the app consumes the flattened DTO directly |
| `activity-feed-source-filter` | done | `core/src/activity.rs` (`ActivitySource`, `list_inner` short-circuits when only one source is wanted) | `ChangesOnly` skips the staging query; `StagingOnly` skips changes; `Merged` runs both. `recent_changes` / `staging_list` commands kept for single-source consumers |

## Sync (sync.md)

File-level multi-device sync over a pluggable transport (libp2p file-blobs / git / none): the wire carries whole-file content + a version hash, never ops. One 3-way text merge (base = last-common version via content-hash history) feeds one unified conflict surface. The default libp2p transport — device identity/enrollment, the Noise-authenticated encrypted channel, mDNS discovery, the zero-knowledge store-and-forward server, auto-sync — is the `hiker-sync` crate. The git transport is `git.md`.

| Slug | Status | Evidence | Notes |
| ---- | ------ | -------- | ----- |
| `sync-path-identity` | done | `hiker-sync/src/{identity,protocol,transport}.rs` (`ManifestEntry { path, current_hash, recent_history_hashes, prior_paths }`; `StateRequest{path}`/`DeltaRequest{path}`/`PushAdopt{path}`; `status_of_path`/`mark_bound` keyed by path), `app/src/sync_service/mod.rs::resolve_fork(path, ...)`, `app/src/panels/sync.rs` (resolution buttons key on `doc.path`) | the transport speaks vault paths end-to-end. A rename is followed via `ManifestEntry.prior_paths` so a receiver still at the old path pulls against the new path. Scenarios + server_sync tests green |
| `sync-concurrent-rename-not-merged` | done | `hiker-sync/src/transport/fork.rs::{resolve_rename_collision,detect_rename_collision,act_on_classification}`; `hiker-sync/src/transport/dialer.rs::sync_entry` (rename-collision block routing); `hiker-sync/src/transport/responder.rs::find_rename_source` (fresh-doc guard) | A concurrent rename-to-same-path now BLOCKS (reason `rename-collision`) for user resolution instead of auto-spawning a loser copy. `detect_rename_collision` still recognizes the disjoint-lineage collision but routes to `resolve_rename_collision`, which records the block + holds (no move/copy/adopt) until the user picks. Resolution converges both devices in one round: **Keep theirs** = peer wins the path, ours moves to a `conflict-` sibling (old auto-behavior, now user-chosen); **Keep mine** = ours keeps the path, we push our base so the peer adopts it + preserve theirs as a `conflict-` sibling; **Keep both** = both survive, loser deterministic by `min(fingerprint)`. Surfaces on the Sync page + attention badge generically (reason flows through `BlockedDoc`). `find_rename_source` fallback now gated on non-empty `prior_paths` so a fresh conflict-copy isn't misread as a rename. Tests: `concurrent_rename_to_same_target_blocks_for_resolution`, `rename_collision_keep_{theirs,mine,both}_converges*`, `non_colliding_rename_does_not_block` |
| `sync-conflict-block-and-resolve` | done | `hiker-sync/src/transport/fork.rs::sync_bound_doc` (same-region block + `resolve_same_region`); `hiker-sync/src/transport/mod.rs::record_blocked`/`blocked_reason` (reason-tagged blocked records); `hiker-sync/src/transport/dialer.rs::sync_entry` (Bound/Blocked gate) | umbrella: same-region edit + fork now block + resolve (reusing the `BlockedDoc`/`Resolution` surface), and clean fast-forward / disjoint-region edits still auto-merge (scenarios `bound_disjoint_edits_still_auto_merge`, `same_region_concurrent_edits_block`, `same_region_keep_{theirs,mine,both}_converges`). Delete-vs-edit now blocks + resolves (Keep-deleted/Keep-edit) via `sync-conflict-delete-vs-edit` (done). Rename-collision now blocks + resolves (Keep mine/theirs/both on path) via `sync-concurrent-rename-not-merged` (done) |
| `sync-conflict-detect-same-region` | done | `core/src/oplog/doc.rs::spans_overlap` (overlap detector, factored out of `three_way_merge`); `core/src/oplog/sync.rs::same_region_verdict` + `SameRegion` enum; `core/src/oplog/meta.rs::most_recent_shared_op_id` (base reconstruction); `hiker-sync/src/transport/fork.rs::sync_bound_doc` (hash-only pre-check + on-demand `fetch_peer_text` + gate) | at delta-apply on a bound doc a 3-way base/ours/theirs span-overlap decides clean-merge vs same-region conflict. base = `materialize_at` of the most-recent content_hash shared by our `.ops` history + the peer's `recent_history_hashes`; ours = `materialize(accepted)`; theirs = the peer's current text fetched on demand (via `DocContentRequest` on the open round connection) only when the cheap hash-only pre-check shows BOTH sides diverged from base — a fast-forward / disjoint-history case pays no fetch. Overlap → block; disjoint / fast-forward → clean 3-way text merge. The detector also flags coincident concurrent insertions (different text at the same anchor) the strict half-open-range test misses, and skips byte-identical twin spans (one side already merged the other's edit → not a conflict). Tests `core::oplog::tests::sync::{disjoint_region_edits_verdict_clean_merge,same_region_edits_verdict_conflict,fast_forward_verdict_clean_merge_without_fetch}` + the scenarios above |
| `sync-conflict-delete-vs-edit` | done | `core/src/oplog/sync.rs::delete_vs_edit_verdict` + `DeleteVsEdit` enum; `core/src/oplog/sync.rs::{tombstone_document_to_trash, adopt_lineage_theirs}` (live→delete trash transition on resolve/adopt); `hiker-sync/src/protocol.rs::DocContentResponse{ text, tombstone }`; `hiker-sync/src/transport/fork.rs::sync_bound_doc` (delete-vs-edit gate) + `resolve_delete_vs_edit`; `hiker-sync/src/identity.rs::Resolution::{KeepDeleted,KeepEdit}`; `hiker-sync/src/transport/dialer.rs::sync_entry` (delete-vs-edit Blocked route); `app/src/panels/sync.rs` (Keep-deleted/Keep-edit buttons on a delete-vs-edit block) | a `Tombstone` concurrent with a `Replace` on a bound doc BLOCKS (reason `delete-vs-edit`) instead of silently delete-winning. Detection: at delta-apply, if EXACTLY one side is tombstoned (XOR of our `materialize_accepted.tombstone` and the peer's, carried on the on-demand `DocContentResponse`), reconstruct the shared base via `most_recent_shared_op_id` + `materialize_at` and compare the LIVE side's text to the base text — a live side that EDITED past the base is a genuine concurrent edit → block; a live side still EQUAL to the base is a sequential fast-forward delete → `NotApplicable`, auto-applies → Phase-3 trash. Both directions block (we-deleted+peer-edited, peer-deleted+we-edited). Resolution: Keep-deleted tombstones+trashes ours then pushes the tombstone (peer adopts via `adopt_lineage_theirs`, ghost trashed on its live→delete transition); Keep-edit resurrects to the live edited text then pushes (peer adopts live) — both converge in one round with no re-block (reuses the keep-mine push/adopt approach). Tests: core `core::oplog::tests::sync::{delete_vs_concurrent_edit_verdict_conflict_both_directions, fast_forward_delete_verdict_not_a_conflict, delete_vs_edit_verdict_not_applicable_when_neither_or_both_tombstoned}`; transport `delete_vs_edit_blocks_both_directions`, `fast_forward_delete_auto_applies_no_block`, `delete_vs_edit_keep_deleted_converges`, `delete_vs_edit_keep_edit_resurrects_and_converges` (scenarios.rs) |
| `sync-conflict-block-persistence` | done | `hiker-sync/src/identity.rs::BlockStore` (`for_vault`/`load`/`save`, JSON at `<vault>/.hiker/sync/blocked.json`); `hiker-sync/src/transport/mod.rs::SyncNode::{new (hydrates blocked + seeds Blocked status), record_blocked, clear_blocked, persist_blocked}`; `core/src/oplog/mod.rs::OpLog::vault_root` (locates the vault dir from the shared handle) | the blocked path + conflict kind + peer fingerprint persist across restart and re-surface: `SyncNode::new` hydrates `blocked` from the per-vault `BlockStore` (and seeds `status` to `Blocked` for each, so `status_of_path` is consistent without a round); every `record_blocked`/`clear_blocked` (the two seams all 4 conflict kinds route through) writes the whole set back through the store, so resolve/converge removes the persisted entry and it does NOT resurrect. Block list is NOT user-scope (not a secret, unlike device/content keys) so it lives next to the op-log it describes.
